3
Fund Descriptions
1. General Fund (1xx) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the general operations of the District.
2. Debt Retirement Funds (3xx) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s debt obligations.
3. Capital Projects Funds (416, 419, 420) – Accounts for the bond proceeds and expenses associated with the District’s bond 
projects.
4. Early Childhood Center Capital Projects Fund (418) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the construction 
of the Early Childhood Center.
5. Sinking Fund (498) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the District’s sinking fund millage.
6. Capital Maintenance Fund (499) – Accounts for the expenses associated with the District’s capital maintenance projects.
7. Food Service Fund (520) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s food service program.
8. TCC Grant Fund (529) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the District’s operations of the Troy Career 
Center.
9. Community Service Fund (530) – Accounts for revenue and expenses associated with the District’s fee for service programs 
including community recreation and CARE.
10. Troy Preschool Fund (531) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the operation of the Troy School District 
Preschool program.
11. Facility Rental Fund (535) – Accounts for the revenue and expenses associated with the District’s rental of various facilities 
including the salaries and benefits of the theater manager. 
12. Troy School and Student Activities Fund (570) – Accounts for the transactions of student groups for school and school-related 
purposes.

2020-21 General Fund Assumptions
• Enrollment assumption: 13,098 (no change from current year)
• Foundation Allowance: 3 scenarios: $200, $400, and $700 reduction in per pupil foundation 
allowance
• Salaries: 
– Adjusted to reflect contractual obligations
– Reduction of 15 (TEA) through attrition
• Benefit increases:
– Health Insurance: 1.65% increase in PA 152 hard cap rate
– Retirement Increase: Increase in the effective rate from 27.50 % to 28.21% 
– FICA rate of 7.65% has not changed 
• Purchased Services increase of approximately $1.2 million or 5% to account for a full year of 
third-party contracts and activity; 2019-20 included adjustments due to the closure of schools 
• Supplies decrease of approximately $1.2 million or 17% as a result of no significant curriculum 
adoption purchases planned for 2020-21; 2019-20 included the adoption of science curriculum

  This  meeting  of  the  Troy  Board  of  Education  was  held 
remotely  via  Zoom  Video  Conferencing.  A  public  notice 
was posted in order to allow for community members to 
participate in this meeting remotely.

p.m.

RECOGNITION  
Troy School District’s Outstanding 
staff employees. This evening we are proud to recognize 
the  accomplishments  of  14  individuals  who  were 
nominated  for  the  Troy  School  District  Outstanding 
Support Staff Award.  The nominees were chosen for this 
distinction by their colleague s, by parents and by former 
and current students, because they make a difference for 
our  students  each  and  every  day.  They  exemplify  our 
values and help us to ensure that when we say we ensure 
learning  for  all  ‐  ALL  truly  means  ALL.    The  following 
individuals  were  congratulated  and  recognized  for  their 
dedication and passion for our students:   Sririsha Bathala 
(Professional Support to Educ ators, Hamilton Elementary 
School),  Thriveni  Boreddy  (Professional  Support  to

Troy School District Retirees

Hamilton Elementary ‐ Dedicated Employee since 1995). 
celebrated May 4th – May 8th.  At tonight’s meeting, the 
Troy School District and Board of Education would like to 
recognize all of our outstanding teachers by adopting the 
following resolution: 
It  was  moved  by  Mrs.  Anne  and  supported  by  Dr. 
Philippart  that  WHEREAS,  throughout  Troy  and  across 
the  country,  teachers  inspire,  nurture  and  open 
children’s minds to new ideas,  
WHEREAS,  teachers  promote  and  strengthen  the 
principles  of  democratic  equa lity,  develop  leaders  and 
encourage critical thinking; and 
WHEREAS, T r o y  t e a c h e r s  s e t  t h e  b a r  f o r  e x c e l l e n c e  i n  
education  and  continually  exceed  the  highest  standards 
in the profession; and 
WHEREAS, t h e  g o a l  o f  T e a c h e r  A p p r e c i a t i o n  W e e k  i s  t o  
raise national and local awareness of teaching excellence 
in building a better society; now,  
THEREFORE  BE  IT   RESOLVED,  that  the  Troy  School 
District  supports  National  Teacher  Appreciation  Week, 
May  4  ‐  8,  2020,  and  encourages  our  schools  and 
community  to  appropriately  recognize  the  vital  and 
changing  role  of  teachers  in  imagining  and  creating  a 
better future for society.

conditions by which the IA is governed and operates; and

I A’s  Joint Steering Co m mittee (“ JSC ”), the JSC adopted a 
Resolution  approving  an  amendment  to  the  governance 
s t r u c t u r e  o f  t h e  I A  a s  s e t  f o r t h  i n  t h e  C o n s o r t i u m

  WHEREAS,  the  boards  of   a  majority  of  the 
Participating  Districts  have  adopted  resolutions  ratifying

adopted by the JSC; and 
  WHEREAS,  the  amended  governance  structure 
provides  that  the  IA  Consortium  shall  be  governed  by  a 
five‐member  body  consisting  of  the  duly  appointed 
representatives  of  its  three  Fiscal  Agents  and  two 
additional  at‐large  members  elected  by  majority  vote  of 
all Participating Districts; and 
  WHEREAS,  with  respect  to  the  initial  election  of 
the  two  at‐large  members,  who  are  to  serve  two‐year 
terms,  the  Fiscal  Agent  representatives  shall  create  a 
ballot  with  the  names  of  the  Superintendents  (or  their 
designees) of  all  Participating  Districts  that  wish  to  have 
representation  on  the  Governing  Body  and  disseminate 
the  ballot  to  the  Participating  Districts’  boards  for  vote; 
and 
  WHEREAS, placement on the ballot shall require a 
resolution  of  a  Participating  District’s  board  designating 
the district’s Superintendent (or his/her designee) as that 
district’s proposed representative as an at‐large member 
of the Governing Body, with the election to take place as 
soon  as  practicable  after  the  effective  date  of  the 
amendment to the governing structure of the IA; and 
  WHEREAS,  the  only  two  Participating  Districts 
whose  boards  have  adopted  a  resolution  designating

member  of  the  Governing  Body  are  Rochester

and  Birmingham  Public  Schools  (Superintendent  Mark 
Dziatczak); and

6.        CONSENT
          D.    CONSIDERATION:       2019-20 Budget Amendment Resolution
Attached is the Budget Amendment Resolution for the 2019-20 fiscal year.  It includes the General Fund - 
Amendment 2, Food Service Fund - Amendment 1, Troy Career Center Fund - Amendment 1, Community Service 
Fund - Amendment 2, Troy School District Preschool Fund - Amendment 2, Facilities Rental Fund - Amendment 
1 , Troy School and Student Activities Fund - Amendment 1, 2016 Capital Projects Fund - Amendment 1, 2019 
Capital Projects Fund - Amendment 1, 2020 Capital Projects Fund - Adoption, Early Childhood Learning Center 
Capital Projects Fund - Amendment 2, and Debt Service Funds - Amendment 1.  Approval of the attached 
resolution is recommended.
We do not have to read the Budget Amendment Resolution in its entirety.  However, it must be made a part of the 
official minutes.
RECOMMENDED ACTION:       Adopt the following resolution.

for the fiscal year 2019-20 for the following funds be amended and approved as attached hereto and 
made a part of the official minutes of the June 16, 2020 regular meeting.

          E.    CONSIDERATION:       2020-21 Budget Appropriations Resolution
Attached is the Appropriations Resolution for the 2020-21 fiscal year.  It includes the General Fund, Food Service 
Fund , Troy Career Center Fund, Adult Education and Community Service Fund , Troy School District Preschool 
Fund, Facilities Rental Fund, Troy School and Student Activities Fund, Capital Maintenance Fund, 2016 Capital 
Projects Fund, 2019 Capital Projects, Early Childhood Learning Center Capital Projects Fund, Sinking Fund 
Capital Projects Fund, and Debt Service Funds.  Approval of the attached resolution is recommended.
We do not have to read the Appropriation Resolution in its entirety.  However, it must be made a part of the 
official minutes.

statement hitherto adopted by the Board.  Changes in the amount appropriated by the 
Board shall require approval by the Board.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Superintendent is hereby charged with general 
supervision of the execution of the budget adopted by the Board and shall hold the
department heads responsible for performance of their responsibilities with the amount
appropriated by the Board of Education in keeping with the budgetary policy statement
hitherto adopted by the Board.
This Appropriation Resolution shall take effect immediately.

6. CONSENT 
F.  CONSIDERATION:  RENEWAL OF FOOD SERVICES CONTRACT 
On May 21, 2019  the Board approved a resolution allowing the 
Administration to enter into a contract with Chartwells to provide the 
food service operation for the district.  The renewal of this contract 
must be approved by the Michigan Department of Education and the 
Troy Board of Education annually.  Approval of t he renewal of  this 
contract by the Michigan Department of Education is forthcoming. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the Troy Board of Education authorize 
administration to approve the contract renewal with Chartwells to provide 
Food Services for the 2020/2021 school year.

 No:

Ms. Saul: 
We are requesting MDE approval to renew our contract with Chartwells for operation of our food 
service program for the 20 20-2021 school year. Per the original RFP, the following items will be 
adjusted: 
1. Meal Equivalent Factor for the 2020 -2021 school year shall be $3.6475 
2. The administration fee will increase by the CPI for the previous January through December 
or by three percent (3%) whichever is less. 
2019-20 Fee: $12,000/mo.,10x        2020-21 Fee: $12,360/mo., 10x   Increase of 3.0% 
3. The management fee will increase by the CPI for the previous January through December or 
by three percent (3%) whichever is  less. 
2019-20 Fee: $0.0500                       2020-21 Fee: $0.0515 Increase of 3.0%

6. G.      06.16.20 
Equity and Excellence Policy - DRAFT  
The Troy School District commits to achieving equity and excellence for each and every student, 
preparing them for citizenship, higher education and the workforce. The School Board will provide 
the leadership and resources such that every student, with support for his or her unique 
circumstances, has access to and is provided the opportunit ies and experiences that lead to 
academic, emotional, physical and social success.  
The Troy School District  believes each student will reach high levels of achievement when 
supported by a challenging curriculum, effective instruction, engaging relationshi ps and an 
environment that promotes their well-being academically, emotionally, physically and socially.  
The Troy School District commits to policies, regulations and practices that incorporate and are 
consistent with the following principles:  
 Developmentally responsive educational practices and inclusive learning environments 
empower students to perform at high levels. TSD  will maintain, and employees will use, 
differentiated teaching strategies, assessment and other tools that support students in their 
learning.   
 Educational outcomes are not presumed by income, race, disability, gender, first language 
or family background.   
 TSD will develop a systematic approach to assessing and monitoring institutional climate, 
ensuring that implicit bias and its potential consequences are understood, and that people of 
diverse backgrounds feel welcome and respected. 
 By working together, administrators, teachers and staff model collaborative behavior to  
improve the quality of learning for all students. Each employee [administrators, teachers 
and staff] is responsible and accountable for the learning and achievement of all students  
and will help build a culture that reinforces this responsibility.   
 Positive educational outcomes are the responsibility of the Troy School District employees 
and our students, in partnership with our families and community.   
TSD will Include teaching and research about racism, its causes and effects, as a central part 
of the teaching mission of our district. 
Our curriculum will strive to 
 Provide students with materials and learning that prepare them to engage respectfully with 
others, regardless of background and prior life experience. 
 Create classroom environments where hierarchy and privilege can be identified and 
challenged and ignorance of barriers to opportunities can be corrected while encouraging 
and supporting freedom of thought and expression. 
 Provide curricular opportunities, as appropriate, in all academic subjects that expose 
students to the role of inequity as it relates to the discipline under study. 
159
 Promote community education and dialogue about racism, its causes and effects, through a 
variety of channels. 
 .

Center.  The present project involves the removal of flooring materials, pipe insulation, caulks and other miscellaneous 
materials.

Contractor for the Troy School District project. 
BID NO. Building Contractor Bid Amount 
1 Niles Center Quality Environmental $117,220.00 
This recommendation is based upon the following factors: 
1. The bid from the Contractor was the low bid. 
2. The Contractor has performed similar projects for numerous school districts. 
3. The Contractor  should be able to provide a safe and effective project within the time parameters of the 
specification.  
For the above noted reasons, along with others, Nova recommends the bid proposal from the above noted Contractor be 
accepted for the project in Troy School District.  Obviously, this recommendation is conditioned upon proper submittals 
from the company in keeping with the requirements of the bid documents and the contract between the School District 
and the Contractor being mutually agreed upon.

  Convergent Technology Partners 6197 Miller Road, Ste #4, Swartz Creek, MI 48473 810.720.3820  E-mail: info@ctpartners.net Website: www.ctpartners.net   
                                              Convergent Technology Partners                                             6197 Miller Rd, Suite 4 Swartz Creek, MI 48502                                             810.720.3820 Fax 810.720.3886                                              E-mail: info@ctpartners.net                                             Website: www.ctpartners.net    
May 28, 2020    Beth Soggs Technology Director Troy School District  Subject: Video Surveillance System RFP # 9900 Recommendation Letter Beth: On March 16, 2020, Troy School District issued a Request for Proposal (RFP) soliciting bids for a Video Surveillance System for the elementary schools and replacement of the system at the middle schools and International Academy, as well as an alternate for the former ITT building.  The RFP was posted to the District’s website.    Convergent also spoke with several vendors regarding the solicitation and pointed them to the website to download the RFP and supporting documentation.  On May 5, 2020, sealed bids were accepted and opened (virtually) by Convergent Technology Partners on behalf of the District. Seventeen proposals were received.  The initial results were tabulated as follows: Bidder  Base Bid Total  ROK SYSTEMS $173,131.58 CBTS $184,958.40 AMCOMM $189,800.00 MASTER ELECTRIC $190,966.22 TRENDSET COMMUNICATIONS $204,145.49 MOSS TELECOMMUNICATIONS $207,869.81 SECURITY 101 $219,641.00 JOHNSON CONTROLS INC $219,840.00 TTI $241,849.41 DETECTION SYSTEMS AND ENGINEERING $250,254.00 SECURITY CORPORATION $255,570.30 SIEMENS $264,458.00 ELECTRONIC SAFETY INC $269,097.00 GEOTECH $275,333.38 BLUESTONE COMMUNICATIONS $299,671.00 PRESIDIO $355,067.55 WADSWORTH SOLUTIONS $400,265.00  All proposals were evaluated for compliance with the RFP requirements, pricing, technical solution and acceptance of terms and conditions of the RFP. Due to the complexity of the RFP bidder quantities of cameras and recording storage size varied. Convergent Technology Partners verified the storage size required for each bidder’s solution with the bidders and revised storage quantities and associated cost, in addition to correcting quantities of cameras, using proposed unit pricing to equalize the bids. 
195
The pool of bidders was reduced to the low eight out of seventeen, using the equalized and revised bid pricing. Note that although Siemens and TTI’s base bid did not fall in this category, their voluntary alternates did. All others have perpetual licenses and software assurance.  The PELCO and Milestone solutions were included for comparison purposes only. Upon including the desired alternates the final short list was compiled for interviews and product demonstrations, that were conducted the week of May 18th: 
BIDDER PRODUCT PRICE TOTAL PRICE W/ ALTERNATES 2 & 3  TOTAL PRICE W/ ALTERNATES 1,2,3 SIEMENS Vol Alternate Axis camera station $ 170,100.00  $178,439.00 $ 192,316.00 ROK SYSTEMS Axis camera station $ 175,696.58 $180,581.57 $ 188,907.23 TTI Vol Alternate Axis camera station $ 191,984.03 $199,460.12 $ 206,864.90 CBTS Milestone X protect $ 204,100.68 $208,719.51 $ 207,076.57  The following factors showed the Axis Camera Station platform and ROK to be a better fit for the district both short and long term: • ROK is low bid when including the chosen alternates • The ROK proposal provides ~60% more storage than the Siemens proposal • One time cost for Software Assurance  • Lower cost of licenses that will provide a cost savings when it’s time to bring the existing HS cameras onto the system o There are approximately 335 existing cameras between the two high schools • Ease of use for end users Based on these factors, we recommend the award of the Video Surviellence System (RFP #9900) project to ROK Systems in the amount of $180,581.57.

This includes: • The award of Alternate 2, two additional cameras (one each building) on the face of Boulan Park and Larson Middle Schools to increase coverage of the parking lot, in the amount of $3,426.82 • The award of Alternate 3, addition of 32 GB local storage at each camera, in the amount of $1,458.17 We also recommend the award of Alternate 1 for including Video Survilience the former ITT building, in the amount of $8,325.75 Lastly, we recommend a 5% contingency of $9,445.37 for a project total, including the former ITT building, of $198,352.69. Memorandum 
 TO:    Rick West, Assistant 
           Superintendent, Business Services 
Technology 
Services 
 FROM: Beth Soggs, Director of Technology 
4420 Livernois 
Phone: 248.823.5060 
 DATE: May 27, 2020 
The AM30 appliances (mini-computers) that are installed on the back of the Smart Displays purchased in May 
2017, need to be upgraded.  Smart is discontinuing updates for the current hardware.  SMART has a 
promotion in place through June 30th for schools to save $250 per appliance.   
Quotes were received from three vendors for 150 AM40 appliances. 
VSC, Inc.  $37,500 
Tierney  $36,375 
AVI SPL  $71,850 
While it is unfortunate that SMART is discontinuing support, we need to do this upgrade since we plan on the 
display devices being in use in the classrooms for the next 6-7 years.  In addition to continuing to receive 
updates from SMART, the updated appliances will allow the Smart displays to better connect to our wireless 
network.  The current appliances only support 2.4 Ghz WiFi, but the new AM40 appliance has dual antennas 
and supports both 2.4 Ghz and 5 Ghz.   This improvement will also improve the ability for staff to AirPlay to 
the devices. Unfortunately, when the purchase was made in 2017, it was the end of that product cycle.  
It is recommended to purchase 150 AM40 Appliances for our SMART displays from Tierney in the amount of 
$36,375.

1. Applicability of Terms 
These General Terms and Conditions (the “Terms and Conditio ns”) are the only

quote (the “Proposal”) by Audio Visual Innovations, Inc.  or Signal Perfection, Ltd.  
(as applicable, “Seller”) to the buyer/customer identified in the Proposal (“Buyer”). 
The Terms and Conditions and the Proposal (collectively, the “Agreement”) 
comprise the entire agreement between the parties, and supersede all prior or 
contemporaneous understandings, agreements, negotiations, representations and 
warranties, and communications, both written and oral. In the event of a conflict 
between these General Terms and Conditions and a valid Customer Care  Service 
Agreement applicable to the Products t hat are the subject of the Proposal , the 
Customer Care Service Agreement shall prevail. In the event of a conflict between 
these General Terms and Conditions and an addendum attached hereto, the 
addendum shall prevail.  In the event of a conflict between t hese General Terms 
and Conditions and any of the other documents constituting the Agreement, these 
General Terms and Conditions shall prevail unless expressly  provided otherwise 
herein. Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, if a master services 
agreement signed by both parties is in effect covering the sale of the Products that 
are the subject of the Proposal , the terms and conditions of said agreement shall 
prevail to the extent they are inconsistent with these Terms and Conditions. 
2. Acceptance of Terms  
This Agreement shall not be binding upon Seller until accepted by Buyer as set forth 
in this section and the earlier of Seller’s confirmation in writing of Buyer’s order and 
Seller’s performance under the applicable Proposal. Buyer’s signed acceptance of 
the Proposal, issuance of order against the Proposal, payment fo r any of the 
Products contained in the Proposal, or receipt of the Products contained in the 
Proposal, whichever occurs first, shall constitute Buyer’s acceptance of this 
Agreement.  Any modification, addition to, or waiver of any of this Agreement shall 
not be effective unless in writing and signed by an authorized representative of 
Seller, and any different or conflicting terms appearing in Buyer’s purchase order or 
other documents are expressly rejected by Seller. No relaxation, forbearance or 
indulgence by Seller in enforcing any of the terms and conditions of this Agreement 
or the granting of any time to any other party shall prejudice or restrict the rights 
and powers of Seller hereunder, nor shall waiver of any breach hereof operate as a 
waiver of any subsequent or continuing breach hereof.   
3.  Delivery 
Seller will use its best efforts to deliver the Products in accordance with the Buyer 
requested delivery date subject to receipt of all necessary information from Buyer.  
Shipping dates are approximate only, and Seller shall not be liable for delays or for 
failure to manufacture due to causes beyond its reasonable control or due to 
compliance with any government regulations. 
Any delay shall extend delivery dates to the extent caused thereby.  Buyer shall 
reimburse Seller its additional expenses resulting from any Buyer -caused delay. 
When delivery of the Products is delayed at the request of the Buyer and the 
Products have already been shipped to Seller, Seller will place the Products in 
storage and Seller will immediately invoice the Buyer 70% of the price, which will 
be promptly paid. Seller shall not be liable, and the Buyer shall have no right to 
cancel or rescind this Agree ment, in the event of any delay due to causes beyond 
Seller’s reasonable control, and Buyer shall accept such delayed performance by 
Seller.  The Buyer’s receipt of the Products shall constitute a waiver of any claims 
for delay.  
4.  Payment Terms 
Unless otherwise agreed in writing by Buyer and Seller, the total Proposal price shall 
be billed as follows: 100% at time of order, payable net 30 from Buyer’s receipt of 
invoice.  Unless otherwise specified, Products are sold F.O.B. origin-Buyer to pay all 
shipping charges. If this Proposal covers Products for more than one system, room, 
suite, or location, for purposes of payment in accordance with payment terms 
stated on the face hereof each room, suite, or location shall be treated as if the 
subject of a separate sale and payment made accordingly.  Seller shall not be liable 
for failures of or delays in manufacture  or delivery resulting from any cause or 
causes beyond its reasonable control. 
5. Buyer in Arrears or Default 
In the event Buyer is in arrears with any payment whatsoever due from it to Seller 
at any time, whether in respect of the Proposal price or any other amount due from 
the Buyer to the Seller under the terms of this Agreement, the amount in arrears 
shall bear interest at the rate of 1.5% per month or the maximum rate permitted by 
applicable law, whichever is less, as from the date each amount falls due, pending 
actual payment thereof in full, without prejudice to any relief or remedy available 
to Seller. In the event of Buyer’s default, Seller may, without notice, peaceably enter 
any premises in which the Products are located and remove, hold and sell them in 
accordance with applicable law, to satisfy in whole or in part Buyer’s obligations. 
6. Title, Risk of Loss 
Title and risk of loss or damage to the Products shall pass to Buyer upon delivery, 
subject to the manufacturer’s software license (if applicable) and a purchase money 
security interest retained by Seller in the Products sold and the proceeds thereof 
until payment of all amounts then due to Seller.  Buyer agrees to cooperate with 
Company in the execution and filing of financing statements under the Uniform 
Commercial Code or other documents as Seller reasonably requests to protect its 
security interest.  Risk of loss or damage to the Products or any part thereof shall 
pass to the Buyer upon delivery.

Except as expressly provided otherwise  in this Agreement, Seller shall not be 
responsible for any installation or other services relating to the Products. Buyer 
shall obtain at its expense and keep effective all permissions, licenses, and permits 
whenever required in connection with the installation and/or use of the Products 
and the premises where the Products shall be situated.

Seller warrants that  the Products will be free from defects in materials and 
workmanship for a period of ninety (90) days from delivery or, with respect to 
Products manufactured by a third party, such longer period of time (if any) provided 
by such manufacturer. Seller shall not be liable for nor have any warranty 
obligations with respect to Products that are  in any way misused, altered and/or 
repaired by someone other than a representative of the Seller which, within the 
sole, reasonable  judgment of the Seller, results in an adverse effect, including 
effects upon performance or reliability of the Products. 
8b. Warranty Claims 
In order to make a claim under the limited warranty provided herein, Buyer shall 
promptly notify Seller in writing  and Seller will, subject to the applicable 
manufacturer’s warranty policy, repair or replace such defective Product at no cost 
to Buyer.  Seller will attempt to reply to warranty claims received from Buyer prior 
to 1:00 p.m. within forty-eight hours.  Normal working hours are 8 a.m. to 5 p.m., 
Monday through Friday, excluding legal holidays.  Buyer shall reasonably  and 
promptly cooperate with Seller’s request for information regarding the claim and 
with return of the defective Product if required.

205
Memorandum 
 TO:    Rick West, Assistant 
           Superintendent, Business Services 
Technology 
Services 
 FROM: Beth Soggs, Director of Technology 
4420 Livernois 
Phone: 248.823.5060 
 DATE: June 3, 2020 
The iPad 1:1 program has been implemented for several years in grades 3-8.  In the 2019-20 
school year, iPads were purchased for the 9th grade class.  As we move forward into the 2020-
21 school year, we need to purchase the following iPads.  This purchase aligns with the 
district’s technology vision and prepares staff and students for a learning environment that 
leverages technology in the fall regardless of whether students are in-seat or virtual.   
All iPads purchased will be encased the Logitech ruggedized keyboard combo case.  Both 
the iPads and the cases will be purchased via the REMC contract.  Additionally, teachers will 
be provided with a Logitech Crayon (similar to the Apple Pencil) which is also available on the 
REMC contract.   
In order to provide iPads to all students in grades K -12, we need to purchase iPads for 
students in grades 3, 9, 11, 12, and a portion of students in grades K -2.  Students in other 
grades already have iPads, and there is already a 2:1 iPad ratio in the K -2 classrooms.  A 
total of 5,000 student iPads are neede d, for a total purchase of $1,750,000.  This price 
includes the iPad at $294 and the case at $56. 
We also need to purchase iPads with the keyboard case and Logitech crayon for our teachers. 
A total of 700 iPads are needed for a total cost of $279,965.  This includes the iPad at $294, 
the case at $56, and the Logitech Crayon at $49.95.  
The total Apple purchase is $2,029,965, and this is a budgeted expense in series 3 of the 
technology bond.

208
International Academy 
Consortium Agreement  
Amendments Since April 28, 2020 JSC Meeting 
May 31, 2020 
The International Academy (“IA”) Joint Steering Committee (“JSC”) held a meeting on April 28, 
2020 to discuss the draft amendments to the IA Consortium Agreement.  Superintendents and 
individual board members of all Consortium school districts were able to  ask questions about the 
Agreement.  As a result of the April 28, 2020 meetings, and the questions raised at the meeting, 
the IA Consortium Agreement has been further updated.  
The amendments to the IA Consortium Agreement since the April 28, 2020 JSC mee ting are as 
follows: 
 The Preamble on page 1 of the Agreement was updated to include a reference to the 
Intergovernmental Transfers of Functions and Responsibilities Act, MCL 125.531 et seq. 
 The Definition of “Governing Body” in Article II, Paragraph H was updated to remove a 
reference to “or designee.” 
 Article III, Section 3, Paragraph A was updated, and the phrase “or where appropriate a 
Michigan public post-secondary education institution” was removed. 
 Article III, Section 3, Paragraph D was updated  to include a requirement of a two -thirds 
(2/3) vote of the Governing Body to expel Participating Districts from the Consortium. 
 Article IV, Section1 Paragraph B (1)(c) was updated to allow the Governing Body to hold 
electronic meetings, provided the public can participate in such meetings.  In addition, the 
Paragraph was updated to allow Governing Body members to appoint designees for 
informational purposes, by not for voting on any action item that comes before the 
Governing Body.   Finally, the Paragraph  was updated to include a provision that the 
Bloomfield Hills Campus shall be deemed the principal office for purposes of the Open 
Meetings Act. 
 Article IV, Section 1, Paragraph C  (2)(f) was updated to state that Participating Districts 
are to receive the  tuition rate calculation formula and tuition rates by December 10 th of 
each year. 
 Article IV, Section 1 , Paragraph C (4) was updated to clarify that a Fiscal Agent cannot 
incur debt obligations “on behalf of the Consortium” without obtaining written appr oval 
from Participating Districts at the Campus. 
Page 1 of 2 
209
 Article V, Section 1, Paragraph B was updated to specify the budget obligations of the 
OKMA campus and that mid-year budget amendments are to be presented to Participating 
Districts. 
 Article V, Section 1, Paragraphs C & D were removed because the obligations that were 
contained in those Paragraphs are contained in Paragraph B. 
 Article V, Section 1, Paragraph E was updated to clarify how Hold Harmless Districts’ 
foundation allowances are calculated for the per pupil charge. 
 Article V, Section 1, Paragraph F was updated to state that the contribution offsets against 
the per pupil charge were net of UAAL costs that are funded by the State and any pass 
through funding. 
 Article VI, Section 2 was updated to clarify the employer of the Academy Principal.
